![обложка|156](<Prārthanā/обложка.jpeg>)

Srila Narottama dasa Thakura spent his days and nights relishing the pastimes of Radha and Krishna amongst the devotees. Prarthana and Prema-bhakti-candrika, the two most prominent works of Narottama dasa Thakura, are in reality the outpourings of a pure heart crying in separation from Gauranga Mahaprabhu and Radha-Govinda. The bhajanas in these books are full of humility, eagerness, and anxiety for devotional perfection. Singing these songs causes the mind to reflect the mood of pure devotion, and the heart to feel intense separation from Radha and Krishna.

Prarthana by Srila Narottama dasa Thakura contains many Bengali bhajanas glorifying Sri Guru, Gauranga Mahaprabhu, and Radha-Govinda. His songs are rich blends of sweet music and profound poetry. They represent the pure spiritual emotions of an aspiring devotee, and the ecstasy-filled loving feelings of a perfect saint.

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura used to tell his disciples that to attain the perfection of life they must know, practice and realize the teachings contained within three books: Prarthana, Prema-bhakti-candrika, and Upadesamrta. With its transliterated Bengali and accurate translations, this present English edition of Prarthana is a most pleasing addition to the ever-expanding ocean of nectar now available to sincere souls seeking the essence of spiritual life.

# Prārthanā

## Cover

“So read Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s *Prārthanā*, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s *Prārthanā*. They are very, very valuable for advancement of spiritual understanding, especially Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s *Prārthanā*. So, by reading them, by understanding them, we can understand Kṛṣṇa very easily. Otherwise, it is very, very difficult to understand Kṛṣṇa.” —Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Svāmī Prabhupāda

## Dedication

This book is dedicated To His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Svāmī Prabhupāda, who revealed the treasure-house of Love of God to the Western world. Without him and his mercy, the precious gift of *kṛṣṇa prema-dhana*, the wealth of divine love of Kṛṣṇa would be forever unknown to the Western world.

## Foreword by Mahānidhi Svāmī

Once after taking initiation from Lokanātha Gosvāmī, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sat in a Vraja *nikuṣja* absorbed in *bhajana*. Suddenly Śrīmatī Rādhikā appeared before him and requested: “Your eternal service is to come to My *kuṣja* and stir milk for My *Prāṇavallabha*. Since Campakalatā also does this service, your name will be Campaka-maṣjarī.”

When Lokanātha Gosvāmī heard about this, he became ecstatic, and confirmed that indeed this was Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s eternal service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Toward the end of his life Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura returned to Kheturi grama. He spent his days relishing *hari-kathā* amongst the devotees. Sometimes he would become overwhelmed by intense pangs of separation and start rolling on the ground while sighing in lamentation: “Ha Kṛṣṇa-Caitanya!” “Ha Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa!”

*Prārthanā* and *Prema-bhakti-candrikā*, the two most prominent works of Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, are in reality the outpourings of a pure heart crying in separation from Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu and Rādhā-Govinda. The *bhajanas* in these books are full of humility, eagerness, and anxiety for devotional perfection. Singing these songs causes the mind to reflect the mood of pure devotion, and the heart to feel intense separation from Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

*Prārthanā* by Śrīla Ṭhākura Mahāśaya, as Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura was later known, contains many Bengali *bhajanas* glorifying Śrī Guru, Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, and Rādhā-Govinda. His songs are rich blends of sweet music and profound poetry. They represent the pure spiritual emotions of an aspiring *sādhaka*, and the ecstasy filled *rāsa-bhāvas* of a *siddha* saint, or a perfectly realized *premī-bhakta*.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura used to tell his disciples that to attain the perfection of life they must know, practice and realize the teachings contained within three books: *Prārthanā*, *Prema-bhakti-candrikā,* and *Upadeśāmṛta*. With its transliterated Bengali and accurate translations this present English edition of *Prārthanā* is a most pleasing addition to the ever-expanding ocean of nectar now available to sincere souls seeking the essence of life—*Kṛṣṇa-prema*. We sincerely hope that all humble Vaiṣṇavas will dive into this ocean and float happily in waves of bliss.

## Introduction

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*vande ’haṁ śrī-guroḥ śrī-yuta-pada-kamalaṁ śrī-gurūn vaiṣṇavāṁś ca*  
*śrī-rūpaṁ sāgrajātaṁ saha-gaṇa-raghunāthānvitāṁ taṁ sa-jīvam*  
*sādvaitaṁ sāvadhūtaṁ parijana-sahitaṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanya-devaṁ*  
*śrī-rādhā-kṛṣṇa-pādān saha-gaṇa-lalitā-śrī-viśākhānvitāṁś ca*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master and unto the feet of all Vaiṣṇavas. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī along with his elder brother Sanātana Gosvāmī, as well as Raghunātha dāsa and Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa, Gopāla Bhaṭṭa, and Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī. I offer my respectful obeisances to Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda along with Advaita Ācārya, Gadādhara, Śrīvāsa, and other associates. I offer my respectful obeisances to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Śrī Kṛṣṇa along with Their associates including Śrī Lalitā and Viśākhā.”**

Śrīla Gour Kiśora dāsa Bābājī was once asked how to develop love of God, to which he replied, “It costs only five *annas*.” The inquirer did not know the meaning of Bābājī Mahāraja’s statement. Śrīla Goura Kiśora Bābājī repeated himself, and then told the inquirer to go to the market with five *annas* and purchase two books by Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, *Prārthanā* and *Śrī Prema-bhakti-candrikā*. He instructed that by reading and meditating on the contents of these two books daily, one will develop love of God.

The songs of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura are accepted by great Vaiṣṇava authorities like Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, as the Vedic evidence. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura accepted the songs of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura to be as good as the *Vedas*.

In one of his writings, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura indicated that to propagate pure *śrī-kṛṣṇa-bhakti*, the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana wrote vast amounts of scriptures according to the order of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In the same way, following in the footsteps of the Gosvāmīs, Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura and Śrīla Bhaktivinoda had composed innumerable songs. While the books of the Gosvāmīs were composed in the Sanskrit language, and are often difficult for the common masses to comprehend, Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura and Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, out of compassion for the fallen people of this age, composed their songs in the local language, namely in simple Bengali. These songs are very sweet, and are very pleasing to the ear. By singing and listening to these songs, one can discover the lost treasure of pure love of God and the process of attaining that love. A steel-framed heart will melt upon singing and hearing repeatedly, those songs that were composed by a great *ācārya* like Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura.

*Prārthānā* is a collection of devotional prayers that are categorised into three sections. These are:

***Samprārthanātmika*** or prayer with wholehearted submission of mind, body, and everything to the Lord. An example of *samprāthanātmika* is given in the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*, 1.2.153 as follows:

*yuvatīnam yathā yūni yūnāñca yuvatau yathā*  
*mano ’bhiramate tad van mano ’bhiramatām tvayi*

**“O my Lord! As the minds of young boys and girls remain attached to one another, please let my mind be attached to You.”**

***Dainyabodhikā*** or making known one’s insignificance and worthlessness. In the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*, 1.2.154 it is cited as follows:

*mat-tulyo nāsti pāpātmā nāparādhī ca kaścana*  
*parihāre ’pi lajjā me kiṁ vruve puruṣottama*

**“O Puruṣottama! In this world, there is no sinner and offender like me. Even though You are an ocean of causeless mercy, I am ashamed even to request You to forgive my offenses. What more shall I say?”**

***Lalasā-mayī*** or an intense yearning for the personal service of the Lord. This is further stated as follows in the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* 1.2.156:

*kadāhaṁ yamunā-tīre nāmāni tava kīrtayan*  
*udvāspaḥ puṇḍarīkākṣa! racayiṣyāmi tāṇḍavam*

**“O Puṇḍarīkākṣa (lotus-eyed Lord)! When, upon the bank of the Yamunā, with eyes brimming with tears of ecstasy and voice choked up with divine spiritual emotion, will I chant Your holy names and dance like a madman?”**

The above verses from *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* are examples of prayers made by devotees at various stages of *sādhana-bhakti*. *Lālasā-mayi* prayers are made by a devotee in whom attachment for the Lord is already manifest. *Samprārthanātmika* prayers are made by a devotee in whom attachment to the Lord is not yet manifest. These are prayers for awakening of one’s attachment. *Dainyabodhikā* prayers are expressions of feelings of humility and one’s utter worthlessness for having not worshiped the Supreme Lord.

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura was a devotee of the highest order. But he composed the *prārthanā* placing himself on the level of a conditioned soul who is crying out for the mercy of the Lord, because of his forgetfulness since time immemorial. Thus various moods are displayed throughout the collections of these 57 prayers. The recitations and singing of these prayers will invoke dormant loving feelings towards Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Great care and effort has been taken in presenting this publication: *Prārthanā* of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura. At least five different copies of the original Bengali editions were used in this presentation. Each of these books differ in terms of how many songs were in *Prārthana* and some lines in some songs have different readings, though they convey the same meaning. Working closely with native Bengali speakers, especially the chief translator of this book, Bhumipati dāsa, who is a Bengali disciple of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Svāmī Prabhupāda, I firmly believe that this translation is true to the original meanings of the compositions of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura. The compilations of *Prārthanā* which I am presently undertaking, is actually for my own *bhajana* and purification. I pray that the Vaiṣṇava communities will be pleased with this publication and grant their mercy on me so I can advance on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Īśvara Dāsa.  
Vṛndāvana, India. Kartika, 1998.

## 1: The Life and Times of Paramahaṁsa Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura Mahāśaya

The biography of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura is fully described in *Bhakti-Ratnākara, Narottama-vilasa, Prema-vilasa* and *Narottama-carita*. The following is a summary of the life and times of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura taken from the above four mentioned books.

In *Narottama-vilasa*, compiled by Śrīla Narahari Ṭhākura, it is narrated as follows: “One day while Caitanya Mahāprabhu was dancing with His associates during *saṅkīrtana*, He suddenly looked in the direction of Śrī Kheturī village. A peculiar look appeared on His face, tears swelled in His eyes, and He cried out the name “Narottama! Narottama!” again and again. The ocean of mercy, Nityānanda Rāya shouted in joy, and Haridāsa, Vakreśvara, and others were inexplicably filled with happiness. Witnessing the unusual behavior of the Lord, the devotees began talking amongst themselves: “Prabhu is repeatedly calling out the name of someone called Narottama.” “It seems that this Narottama is extremely dear to the Lord, perhaps he is about to take birth soon.” “Oh who is that fortunate man who will have such a son?” “The woman who bears him within her womb is the most fortunate lady in the world.” “Obviously, the Lord has plans for this Narottama to fulfill His mission.” In this way the rumors of the appearance of Narottama spread far and wide.

On the banks of the Padmāvatī river, in the town of Gopālpura lived King Kṛṣṇānanda Datta. His older brother was Puruṣottama Datta. The wealth and fame of these two brothers was beyond compare. In the month of Magha, on the full moon day, circa 1531 AD, Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura took his birth. Seeing many auspicious omens surrounding the birth of his son, Rāja Kṛṣṇānanda was very pleased, and gave lavishly in charity to the brāhmaṇas. The brāhmaṇas, seeing all the auspicious signs surrounding the child’s birth, prophesised that the boy was a highly perfected soul and a great personality, by whose influence many people would be delivered. Just as a crescent moon gradually becomes full the king’s son grew more effulgent and beautiful with each passing day. His bodily luster was just like molten gold. His eyes were large and shaped like lotus petals. His arms extended to his knees and he had a deep navel. All these are the bodily symptoms of a mahāpuruṣa, a great personality. All the local townspeople used to gather to take darśana of the beautiful and saintly child. Soon, the boy’s anna-praśana, or grain-eating ceremony was held. At that time, in order to insure auspiciousness and a bright future for his son, King Kṛṣṇānanda gave much charity.

The king’s wife’s name was Nārāyāṇī Devī. Having given birth to such a wonderful son, she was floating in the ocean of ecstasy. She always kept the boy close to her and was always concerned for his well-being, caring for him constantly. The boy was extremely peaceful. Wherever his mother put him, he would quietly stay put. Within the ladies quarters, the ladies used to nurture and care for the child with great ecstasy. Gradually the day of his entrance into school (hate-khari) approached. As his schooling went on, everyone was astonished at his amazing intelligence. He was a śrūtidhara, simply by hearing a subject once from his gurus he would commit it to memory. In only a short time, he mastered the study of Sanskrit grammar, poetry, rhetoric, and became expert in many different scriptures. But gradually he saw as useless any knowledge which did not promote hari-bhajana, and soon lost interest in everything but Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He found that in previous times the learned, who understood what real knowledge is, left everything, renouncing the world of birth and death, and went off to the forest in order to worship the Supreme Lord, Hari. Day after day, Narottama began to think of how he could become free from family life and constantly engage himself in the Lord’s service. He was indifferent to all material enjoyment; where most children usually like to play, he was uninterested in playing games. At this time, hearing the glories of Śrī Nityānanda and Śrī Gaurāṅga from the lotus lips of the devotees of the Lord, his heart was filled with spiritual joy. As if he had been given new life, he felt great ecstasy. Within a few days of hearing the wonderful qualities of Gaura and Nityānanda, he began to chant Their holy names in japa day and night. One night the merciful Śrī Gaurasundara, surrounded by His eternal associates, appeared to Narottama in a dream and gave him darśana.

After this, in order to understand how to become free from family life, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura began to think day and night of how he could go to Vṛndāvana. Narottama had prayed, hari hari! kabe haba vṛndāvana vasi? “O Hari, when will I become a resident of Vṛndāvana? When will I serve the beautiful divine couple in Vṛndāvana with tears in my eyes”? In this way, Narottama would constantly sing. Upon seeing Narottama’s indifference to all sense pleasure and his intense renunciation, King Kṛṣṇānanda and Nārāyaṇī devi began to think about what to do with him. Seeing that he was not inclined towards family life, and that he might soon unexpectedly leave home to go to Vṛndāvana, they became very worried. They employed a guard to watch over him so that he could not leave. Narottama dāsa saw that the difficulties involved in escaping were becoming more impassable than the Himalayas, and thought that he might never be able to go to Vṛndāvana and absorb himself in the worship of the holy feet of Śrī Gaurāṅga. Thinking only of how to get the mercy of Śrī Gaura-Nityānanda, he began to pray very earnestly for guidance from them. And so it came to pass that some messengers came from the ruler of Bengal, informing King Kṛṣṇānanda that the ruler of Bengal wanted to meet with him. In order to meet with the ruler of Bengal, Rāja Kṛṣṇānanda and his brother Puruṣottama Datta set out on a long journey to the court.

Narottama took advantage of the moment, thinking it to be a good opportunity to leave home. At that time, he secretly left his mother and his protectors, turned towards Vṛndāvana and began his journey to that Holy Land. It was on the full moon day in the month of Kartika that Narottama Ṭhākura left his family behind. Passing through Bengal in a short time, he soon found himself on the road to Mathurā. All the other pilgrims he would meet were very affectionate toward Narottama, as they could recognize him as the son of the king. He would sometimes live by drinking milk and sometimes would eat the roots and fruits as he went along. His constant hankering to see Vṛndāvana made his hunger and thirst go away. As he moved from place to place, he would hear the glories of Gaura and Nityānanda from the mouths of many devotees. In this way he was always thinking about Their lotus feet, constantly absorbed in meditation. As he walked and walked, he prayed to the lotus feet of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu. He had written: “*āra kabe nitāi chaṅd*....” “When will Nityānanda show His mercy to me and free me from the world of birth and death? By His mercy one may give up material enjoyment and purīfy his mind. In this way, by the mercy of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu, one can attain Vṛndāvana.”

In this way, walking and walking, Narottama dāsa arrived in Mathurā and, upon seeing the Yamunā river, he offered many prayers. By remembering the names of the six Gosvāmīs headed by Śrī Rūpa and Śrī Sanātana, he began to weep in ecstasy. Gradually he came to Vṛndāvana itself and entered into that Holy Land. He was sent by Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī to serve the lotus feet of Lokanātha Gosvāmī. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu had already foretold the initiation of Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura by Lokanātha Gosvāmī before the appearance of Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura. In *Narottama-vilasa*, it is stated: “Within a short period of time a prince named Narottama will become your disciple. He will be a gifted devotee, full of love and devotion, and empowered to save the fallen conditioned souls from hellish life. His enchanting beautiful kīrtana will melt even wood or stone.”

Lokanātha Mahārāja was so deeply agonized by the pain of separation from Śrī Gaurāṅga that it was as if his very life had been taken away. Narottama offered his obeisances to the lotus feet of Śrī Lokanātha and Lokanātha asked him, “Who are you?” Narottama said, “I am your fallen servant. I wish to serve your lotus feet.” Lokanātha replied, “How can I, who am unable to serve Gaura-Nityānanda, accept any service?” It so happened that Lokanātha Gosvāmī was determined not to accept any disciple, so following this vow, he declined to accept Narottama dāsa as his disciple. But Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura was very determined to have the shelter of Śrīla Lokanātha Gosvāmī. After this, Narottama Ṭhākura would secretly go in the middle of the night to the place where Lokanātha Gosvāmī would pass stool and urine. Narottama Ṭhākura would cleanse the spot, maintaining everything there very nicely. After a year of serving in this way, his service was recognized by Lokanātha Gosvāmī, and he bestowed his mercy upon Narottama Ṭhākura. On the full moon day of the month of Śravana, he initiated Narottama Ṭhākura as his disciple.

Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura used to eat by practicing madhukarī, and he would study the Gosvāmī scriptures under the guidance of Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī. Śrīnīvās Ācārya was his dear friend, and together the two of them used to study under Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī. At that time Śyāmānanda Prabhu arrived from Gauḍa-desā, and he also began studying the Gosvāmī’s scriptures under the guidance of Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī. These three, with one mind, performed their bhajana in Vṛndāvana and yet felt that their inner aspiration had not attained its complete fulfillment. One day Śrī Jīva Gosvāmī called the three of them to him and said, “In the future you must preach the message of Śrīman Mahāprabhu far and wide. Taking the literatures of the Gosvāmīs you must quickly go to Bengal and begin preaching.”

The three of them, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, Śrīnīvās Ācārya and Śyāmānanda Prabhu, gave up their residence in Vṛndāvana and accepted the order of their guru on their heads. Taking the treasured scriptures of the Gosvāmīs with them they started on their journey to Bengal. As they continued on their way, gradually they came to Vanaviṣṇupura. In Vanaviṣṇupura lived a king of dacoits and thieves named Śrī Birhambira. At night he had the scriptures stolen, thinking them to be a treasure of some kind. When they awoke in the morning and saw the scriptures stolen, Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, Śrīnīvās Ācārya and Śyāmānanda Prabhu felt as if their heads had been hit by a thunderbolt. Miserable beyond description, they all began searching in all four directions for the scriptures, until finally news came to them that King Birhambira had stolen the books and was keeping them hidden in his royal storehouse. At this, it was decided among the three that Śrī Śyāmānanda Prabhu should head for Utkala, and then Narottama started for Kheturī-grama, while Śrīnivās Ācārya stayed behind, thinking of some ways to deliver the Gosvāmī’s books from the King’s storehouse.

In order to see the holy birthplace of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Narottama went quickly to visit Navadwīpa. Arriving there, he began chanting, “O Gaurahari! O Gaurahari!” on the banks of the Ganges, hundreds and hundreds of times and offered many prayers to the Lord. Sitting beneath the shade of a Tāl tree, he began to wonder, “Where is the actual place of Śrī Caitanya’s birth?” He sat there for some time, thinking of what to see next.

Just then, an old *brāhmaṇa* happened to walk by. Narottama rose to his feet to show respect to the *brāhmaṇa*. The *brāhmaṇa* said, “Bābā, from where have you come? What is your name?” Narottama introduced himself and expressed his desire to see the holy birthplace of Śrī Caitanya.

The *brāhmaṇa* replied, “Today my life has become auspicious, for before my eyes is a dear devotee of Śrī Caitanya.” Narottama said, “Bābā! You have seen Caitanya?” The brāhmaṇa replied, “What are you saying! Every day Nimāi Paṇḍita sits with His disciples at this ghaṭa and discusses the śāstra, and then, from a distance I watch Him and wonder at the beauty of His divine form. Today I remember seeing that very form sitting beneath this tree here, where He sits every day.”

As the *brāhmaṇa* spoke, tears of ecstasy poured from his eyes. Narottama said, “Bābā! It is the fortune of a lifetime that I have beheld you with these eyes.” Saying this, tears fell from his eyes. Falling to the ground, Narottama touched the *brāhmaṇa*’s feet to his head. The *brāhmaṇa* said, “Bābā! I give you my blessings that soon you will attain devotion to the lotus feet of Govinda. At that time, you will preach the glories of Gaura-Govinda far and wide.”

After this, the *brāhmaṇa* showed Narottama dāsa the path to where the house of Jagannātha Miśra was. Finding that path, Śrī Narottama walked until he came to the house of Jagannātha Miśra. Arriving at the house of Jagannātha Miśra, he fell down at the door with tears in his eyes and offered his full obeisances, while reciting various prayers in glorification of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Upon entering within the house he had darśana of the lotus feet of Śuklāmbara Brahmacārī. Narottama offered his respects to his feet.

From different signs, Śuklāmbara Cakravartī could understand that Narottama was an agent of Śrī Caitanya’s mercy. He asked, “Who are you?” Narottama introduced himself, explaining that he had been living in Vṛndāvana under the care of Jīva Gosvāmī and Lokanātha Gosvāmī, and that he had just arrived in Navadwīpa. Śrī Śuklambara Cakravartī said, “Bābā, you have come from Vṛndāvana? You were with Lokanātha and Jīva Gosvāmī?” At length, he made Narottama rise, and embraced him heartily, barraging him with endless questions about the well-being, activities, and good health of the Gosvāmīs. In this way he talked on and on with Narottama, as he wanted to hear all about Vṛndāvana and everything that was going on there.

Finally, Narottama got to meet Iśāna Ṭhākura, the old servant of Śrī Śacīmātā, and upon meeting him, he offered his prayers of respect at his holy feet and introduced himself. Śrī Iśana Ṭhākura touched Narottama’s head, bestowing his blessings upon him, and then embraced him affectionately. After this, Narottama met Śrī Dāmodara Paṇḍita and offered his respects to him. Then, he went to the house of Śrīvāsa Paṇḍita and offered his respects to Śrīpati and Śrīnidhi Paṇḍita. All of them affectionately embraced Narottama.

After staying for a few days in Māyāpura, Narottama went to visit the house of Advaita in Śāntipura. There he offered his respects at the lotus feet of Śrīla Acyutānanda. Śrīla Acyutānanda introduced himself and inquired after the health and well-being of the Vṛndāvana Gosvāmīs. After staying in Śāntipura for two days, he went on to Ambikā Kalna to the house of Gaurīdāsa Paṇḍita. At that time, Śrī Hṛdaya Caitanya Prabhu was staying there. He was the disciple of Gaurī dāsa Paṇḍita. Śrī Narottama offered his respects to Hṛdaya Caitanya. The two of them affectionately embraced and Narottama gave him news of the activities of the Gosvāmīs in Vṛndāvana. After staying in Ambikā Kalnā for a day, he went on to the place where the Ganges, Yamunā, and Sarasvatī met, at a village called Saptagram. In that place Uddhārana Datta Ṭhākura lived. Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu had previously given his mercy to the residents of Saptagram, and so all the people there were great devotees.

After the disappearance of Uddhārana Datta Ṭhākura, the people in the village felt like they had lost their vision. Śrī Narottama went to the house of Uddhārana Datta Ṭhākura and there he found that the devotees were absorbed in separation from their guru, in great agony passing their days. Narottama dāsa offered his respects to all the Vaiṣṇavas there and then continued on his way to Kharadaha Gram. In Kharadaha Gram, Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu had a residence where His two energies, Śrī Vasudhā and Jahnavā Devī lived. Śrī Narottama went to the house of Lord Nityānanda, and remembering the sublime holy name of Lord Nityānanda his voice choked up in ecstasy. Śrī Parameśvarī dāsa Ṭhākura took Śrī Narottama within the inner portion of the house reserved for women and brought him to the lotus feet of Śrī Jahnavā Mātā and Śrī Vasudhā. Having been introduced to Narottama, whom they could understand had received the mercy of Lokanātha and Jīva Gosvāmī, they bestowed their mercy upon him.

It is said in the Bhakti-Ratnakara that both Vasudhā, who was the knower of all truths, and Śrī Jahnavā, who is the Supreme Mistress, bestowed their incomparable mercy upon Narottama.

Four days later, Narottama, after having spent much time discussing kṛṣṇa-katha in great ecstasy with Śrī Jahnavā and Vasudhā, bade them farewell and left for the town of Khanakula Kṛṣṇa-nagara to see the place of Abhirāma Gopāl Ṭhākura. Having met him, Narottama offered his respects to the holy feet of Abhirāma Gopāl. He was passing his days in great pain of separation from Śrī Caitanya and Nityānanda. He had become extremely internal, barely spending any time in the plane of external consciousness. Seeing him in that condition, Narottama wept a great deal. The Gopinātha deity of Abhirāma Ṭhākura was wonderful to see. Narottama took darśana of the deity and recited many prayers and verses in praise of the Lord before the deity. The next day, after taking his leave of the house of Abhirāma Gopāl, Narottama had some inspiration from within and set out for Jagannātha Purī.

Constantly absorbed in thinking of the eternal associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, it seemed to Narottama that he reached Jagannātha Purī in no time at all. Śrī Gopīnātha Ācārya and many other devotees, seeing Narottama on the path, all went out to greet him. In this way, surrounded by devotees, he arrived in Jagannātha Purī. After Śrī Narottama had paid his obeisances at the lotus feet of Gopīnātha Ācārya and the Ācārya had warmly embraced him, Gopīnātha Ācārya said, “This very day I was hoping that you might arrive.” Soon Narottama and all the devotees from Vṛndāvana and Bengal began talking on and on about Kṛṣṇa, Gaurāṅga, the Gosvāmīs and many other things. All the devotees were very happy to have Narottama in their midst, and they took him to have darśana in the temple of Jagannātha.

While taking darśana of Jagannātha, Bāladeva and Subhadrā, Narottama offered many fine prayers in their glorification and bowed down before them again and again.

After this he went to the house of Haridāsa Ṭhākura and upon arriving there, he began to drown in the ocean of śrī-kṛṣṇa-prema. After this he went to the house of Gadādhara Paṇḍita, and began to shout at the top of his lungs in a loud voice, “O life and soul of Gaurāṅga! O Gadādhara!” Thereafter he took darśana of the Toṭa Gopīnātha Deity and offered his respects at the lotus feet of Śrī Mamu Gosvāmī Ṭhākura who was at that time engaged in the service of Śrī Gopīnātha.

Thereafter, Śrī Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura drew near to that part of the Gopīnātha deity where it is said that Mahāprabhu had entered into, in the presence of His devotees. This is recorded in Bhakti-Ratnakara as follows: “It is difficult to understand the movements of that crown jewel of sannyāsīs, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Upon His disappearance, all of a sudden the earth became dark. He entered into the Gopīnātha mandira and never came out. In this way He left the visible plane and entered into the invisible plane.” (*Bhakti Ratnakara*8:357).

Upon hearing of the disappearance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Narottama cried out: “O Śacīnandana, O Gaurahari!” and fell unconscious on the ground. Seeing Narottama’s bewildered state of separation from the Lord, all the devotees began crying tears of prema. After this, Narottama went to the house of Kāśī Miśra, took darśana of the generous lotus feet of Śrī Gopāla Guru Gosvāmī and saw the deities at the Rādhā-Kanta temple. After this he went to the Guṇḍicā temple for darśana, and remembered the Lord’s pastimes in the Jagannātha-vallabha gardens. Then he visited the Narendra Sarovara, and many other places. After a few days, having experienced great transcendental bliss in the association of the devotees of Jagannātha Purī, and having visited all the places of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s pastimes, he took his leave from the devotees and left for Śrī Nṛsiṁhapura. Once there, he went to Śyāmānanda Prabhu’s house. Seeing Narottama after such a long time, Śyāmānanda Prabhu was floating in the ocean of ecstasy. In the ecstasy of kṛṣṇa-prema the two of them joyfully embraced one another.

Śyāmānanda Prabhu, out of affection kept Narottama in Nṛsiṁhapura for many days, not allowing him to leave. Śrī Narottama sanctified the town of Nṛsiṁhapura by drowning everyone there in a nectarine flood of *Saṅkīrtana*. Together Śyāmānanda and Narottama would go on discussing kṛṣṇa-katha in great ecstasy, without any knowledge of whether it was day or night. After some time, Śrī Narottama Ṭhākura bade farewell to Śrī Śyāmānanda Prabhu and set out for Gauḍa-deśa.

Narottama Ṭhākura quickly came to Śrī Khanda. There he offered his respects to the lotus feet of Śrī Narahari Sarakāra Ṭhākura and Śrī Raghunandana. Śrī Narahari Sarakāra Ṭhākura had great affection for Narottama’s father, Śrī Kṛṣṇānanda. Offering his respects to Narottama in return, Narahari blessed him many times, touching him on the head with his hand. Śrī Raghunandana embraced him affectionately. They asked him to tell them all about the devotees in Jagannātha Purī. In this way, Narottama went from one place to the next in Śrī Khanda, his transcendental pleasure always increasing. Narottama spent many days in Śrī Khanda in great happiness, performing *Saṅkīrtana* and dancing in the association of the devotees there.

Bidding farewell to all the eternal associates of Mahāprabhu in Śrī Khanda, he went on to Kanthak Nagara, to the house of Gadādhara dāsa Ṭhākura. He fell before the house of Gadādhara dāsa, offering his respects, and Gadādhara dāsa affectionately embraced him. The Bhakti Ratnakara says, “Seeing Narotttama, Gadādhara Dāsa embraced him with great affection, wetting his body with tears of joy.” Śrī Gadāhara Dāsa Prabhu was passing his days in pain of separation from Gaura-Nityānanda. Narottama Ṭhākura stayed there for two days, and then left to see the birthplace of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu in Rādhā-deśa.

Narottama went to Ekachakra Gram to visit the holy place of Nityānanda’s birth. There, an old *brāhmaṇa* who was affectionately disposed towards Narottama showed him the different holy places where Nityānanda had performed His līlā. Remembering the holy names of Haḍāi Paṇḍita and Padmavatī devī, Narottama fell to the ground, his voice choked up with ecstasy. After seeing the holy birthplace of Nityānanda, Narottama started out for Kheturī Gram.

Bhakti Ratnakara says, “After inquiring about the way to Kheturī Gram from the local people, Narottama quickly came to the banks of the Padmavatī. Crossing over the Padmavatī, he arrived in Kheturī. Upon his arrival, all the people of the village wanted to be the first to meet him.”

Since Narottama had been away for so long, there was a big welcoming celebration. Hearing of his arrival, the residents of Kheturī Gram all began making preparations to welcome him. Rāja Kṛṣṇānanda and Śrī Puruṣottama Datta, the father and uncle of Narottama had already passed away, and had gone back to the spiritual world. Puruṣottama Datta, Narottama’s uncle had a son whose name was Śrī Santoṣa Datta. After the passing of King Kṛṣṇānanda and Puruṣottama, he was shown much wealth and material enjoyment. Santoṣa was the foremost of saintly persons in the village. When he heard that Narottama Ṭhākura was about to bless that town with his holy footsteps after such a long time, in order to be the first one to greet Narottama, he rushed out with the rest of the devotees to wait for him on the road outside Kheturī Gram.

After some time, Narottama could be seen in the distance. Upon seeing him, Santoṣa, after bowing prostrate with full obeisances, went forward with tears of ecstasy in his eyes, falling on the ground again and again to take the dust of Narottama’s lotus feet. At this, Narottama affectionately embraced Santoṣa and asked how he had been faring all this time. Narottama asked King Santoṣa many questions about his health, happiness and well-being. A few days later, Narottama Ṭhākura initiated Santoṣa with the rādhā-kṛṣṇa mantra. Rāja Santoṣa Datta had previously wanted that a temple be built and a deity installed. Now he begged at Narottama Ṭhākura’s lotus feet for his permission. Narottama gladly gave his approval.

Within a few months, Rāja Santoṣa Datta had seen to it that a large temple was built. The temple compound included a big storehouse for food, a kīrtana hall, a residence hall and aśrāma for devotees, a bathing pond, a beautiful flower garden, and a guesthouse. On the full moon day of Phalguna on the festival day of Mahāprabhu’s appearance, the temple was dedicated, the deities were installed, and a great festival, which can only be compared to the rājasuya-yajña of Yudhiṣṭira Mahārāja in its vast expansiveness, began and was celebrated for miles around. A Messengers were sent out for miles around Kheturī Gram, to near and distant lands, to invite kings, landowners, poets, Paṇḍitas, Vaiṣṇavas, authors, as well as many other illustrious guests. Some were sent far and wide to invite the topmost singers and orators. Preparations were made to install six deities at one time.

## 2: The Great Festival of Kheturī Gram

In Buddhari Gram, at the house of Govinda Kavirāja, the news came of the festival. All the devotees there along with Śrīnivāsa Ācārya soon set out for an auspicious visit to the great festival in Kheturī Gram. Within a few days, from Nṛṣīṁhapura in Orissa came Śrī Śyāmānanda Prabhu, from Khoradoha came Śrī Jahnavā Mātā and Śrī Parameśvarī dāsa, Kṛṣṇadāsa Sarakhel, Madhava Ācārya, Raghupati Vaidya, Minaketana Rāma dāsa, Murari Caitanya dāsa, Jñāna dāsa, Mahidhara, Śrī Śaṅkara, Kamala Kara Pippalai, Gaurāṅga dāsa, Nakari, Kṛṣṇa dāsa, Dāmodara, Balarāma dāsa, Śrī Mukunda, and Śrī Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura. From Śrī Khanda came Śrī Raghunandana and many other devotees, from Navadwīpa came Śrī Pati, Śrī Nidhi and others, from Śantipura came Advaita Ācārya Prabhu’s son Acyutānanda , Śrī Kṛṣṇa Miśra, and Śrī Gopāl as well as many others. From Ambikā Kalna came Śrī Hṛdaya Caitanya Prabhu and many other Vaiṣṇavas. Gradually they all arrived in Kheturī Gram. King Santoṣa Datta arranged a colossal boat to carry everyone across the river. On the other side of the river, he had arranged for palanquins, ox-carts and other conveyances to carry the devotees into Kheturī Gram. Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Narottama Ṭhākura, and Rāja Santoṣa, went forward to cordially meet the devotees, and after showing them all due respect, they offered the devotees flower garlands, congratulated and welcomed them with great affection. All the devotees were given separate houses and servants. All those great Vaiṣṇavas, who are the saviors of the world, graced the land of Kheturī Gram with the dust of their lotus feet, thus transforming it into a great place of pilgrimage. The Saṅkīrtan of those great souls created a thunderous sound that filled the heavens.

The gates of the temple and the doors of the houses were all well decorated with banana leaves, waterpots, auspicious things like mango leaves, small waterpots decorated with auspicious signs, and different kinds of flowers. All the big gateways and ornamental arches in the city, as well as all the doors of all the houses were decorated with colorful auspicious symbols like śvastikas and six-pointed stars. The whole effect was one of unprecedented beauty and enchantment. Just before the festival stage in different places were mountains of all kinds of earthen pots, all kinds of silver vessels, and gigantic containers filled with milk, ghee, and thousands of earthen pots filled with yogurt. All these waterpots, earthen pots, silver vessels and giant containers as well as giant heaps of vegetables, produce, and fruit that were to be prepared for the festival combined to give the appearance of a great and beautiful mountain.

The day before the installation ceremony, the devotees, on the order of Śrī Jahnavā Mātā, began making the final preparations for the installation of the Deities on the holy appearance day of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. That evening they began the preliminary celebratory ceremonies by performing *Saṅkīrtana* after Narottama Ṭhākura had first offered candana and flower garlands to Śrī Jahnavā Mātā and properly worshiped her. Thereafter all the devotees were decorated with garlands, and upon the request of Śrī Narottama and Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Śrī Raghunandana Ṭhākura sang the maṅgalācarana, the auspicious invocation. On and on into the middle of the night the devotees performed saṅkīrtana in order to make the atmosphere auspicious for the great festival that was to follow. After chanting and dancing into the middle of the night the Vaiṣṇavas finally took rest. Many thousands of people honored mahā-prasāda that night, on the eve of the great festival of Kheturī Gram.

The next morning, the installation ceremonies and the celebration of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s appearance, began with all the Vaiṣṇavas performing mahā-saṅkīrtana. Śrīnivāsa Ācārya presided over the auspicious abhiṣeka ceremony for the six deities that were being installed. About an hour before Śrīnīvās Ācārya was to perform the abhiṣeka, the six deities arrived within the temple. At that time, the local devotees, as well as those who had come from far away also arrived. The orators spoke, and the singers sang sweet songs. The expert dancers performed wonderful dances. The different devotees filled the four directions with the ecstatic sounds of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, and the glories of the Lord. In this way the four directions were filled with ecstasy.

According to the appropriate rules and regulations of the śāstras, Śrīnivāsa Ācārya performed the abhiṣeka ceremony, after which the deities were dressed in the finest cloth, and decorated with beautiful ornaments. After this, different kinds of sweet rice and wonderful vegetable preparations and drinks and thousands of varieties of foodstuffs were offered to the deities. The six deities, Śrī Gaurāṅga, Śrī Vallabhikanta, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Vrajeṅdramohan, Śrī Rādhā Ramana, and Śrī Rādhā-Kanta, were offered all manner of wonderful preparations of bhoga in different pots. As the bhoga was offered, kīrtana was performed, and then after the deities were satisfied in taking all the preparations to Their heart’s content. They were offered a soothing mouthwash of tāmbūla, and then the deities were decorated with fragrant sandalwood pulp and garlands. After this there was a great arati, during which, in great ecstasy all the devotees performed *saṅkīrtana*. After performing kīrtana and dancing in great bliss, all the devotees, forgetting their material bodies, fell to the ground offering prostrated daṇḍavats. After this, Śrīnivāsa Ācārya offered the prasādam sandalwood pulp and garlands to Jahnavā Mātā. Thereafter, he gave garlands and sandalwood pulp to all the devotees present. Śrīnivāsa, Śrī Narottama, and Śrī Śyāmānanda Prabhu distributed all the sandalwood pulp and garlands to all the devotees present, and when they were finished, on the order of Jahnavā Mātā, Śrī Nṛsiṁha Caitanya dāsa decorated Śrīnivāsa, Śyāmānanda and Narottama with flower garlands and sandalwood pulp. Gradually the devotees took their seats in the kīrtana hall. Jahnavā Mātā was seated on an elevated chair at the front of the hall. At that time, on the order of Jahnavā Mātā and Acyutānanda, Śrī Narottama Ṭhākura Mahāśaya began to perform kīrtana. Śrī Gaurāṅga dāsa, Śrī Gokula dāsa, and Śrī Vallabha dāsa responded and Devidāsa played the mṛdānga. The sweet and wonderful kīrtana of Narottama Ṭhākura, with its sweet tune and unique style of voice, filled the four directions and the heavens above, causing all the men and women to weep tears of prema, and they all sported in the waves of the Vaikuṇṭha ocean of ecstasy. At that time, Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, along with His own personal associates appeared there and joined in the *saṅkīrtana*.

“Who can describe the wealth of happiness felt by the devotees when, in the midst of their kīrtana, appeared the munificent Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself. Like a flash of lightening in the midst of a mass of beautiful clouds, Śrī Caitanya Himself appeared within the crowd of devotees through divine manifestation.” (*Bhakti Ratnakara* 10: 572)

Mahāprabhu appeared in their midst along with Śrī Narahari, Śrī Mukunda, Śrī Gaurī dāsa Paṇḍita, Śrī Advaita Ācārya, Nityānanda, Madhava Ghosh, Vasu Ghosh, Govinda Ghosh, Ācārya Pūrandara, Śrī Maheśa, Śrī Śaṅkara, Śrīdhara, Śrī Jagadiśa Paṇḍita, Śrī Yadunandana, Śrī Kaśiśvara, and many of the Lord’s eternal associates. They danced in a large company, with a cast of thousands, and in their midst, Śrī Acyutānanda, Śrī Raghunandana, Śrī Pati, and Śrī Nidhi as well as many others joined together in the intense devotional ecstasy of that great kīrtana of singing and dancing.

“How overwhelmed in ecstasy were Advaita and Nityānanda! How wonderful was the circle the devotees made around Śrī Caitanya, who danced in its middle. Śrīnivāsa Ācārya and Narottama Ṭhākura fully understood these pastimes as the wonderful manifestation of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s causeless mercy! It was an act of grace shown to Śrīnivāsa Ācārya and Narottama Ṭhākura. With this their satisfaction was complete.” (*Bhakti Ratnakara* 10: 607)

When Mahāprabhu, who is most affectionate to all devotees, appeared with His own eternal associates, Śrīnivāsa Ācārya and Narottama Ṭhākura felt complete joy and satisfaction. After the *saṅkīrtana*, Śrī Jahnavā Mātā offered the deities some red powder, which is usually thrown by Hindus at the time of the Holi festival. After this, upon her order, the devotees began to play with the dye powder by throwing it all over each other in great joy. Everyone’s body was covered with the red powder as the devotees threw it on each other. In this way, after the fun they had with the red dye powder, the evening approached and the time came to celebrate the appearance of Śrī Gaurāṅga with an abhiṣeka ceremony. When twilight came, the bathing ceremony began and it was conducted by Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, and the devotees sang the abhiṣeka song for Mahāprabhu’s appearance ceremony and celebration. The translation to the song went like this:

“At an auspicious time during the full moon evening of the month of Phalguna, Kṛṣṇa, the Lord of Gokula made His appearance within the town of Nadiya as the ocean of ecstasy rose, flooding every house. At that time, everyone had great fun with one another. Śacīdevī was healthy and happy as the baby played on her lap within the house of Jagannātha Suta. Morning, noon, evening, and night everyone wanted to see the child, to touch Him and hold Him. Accepting all kinds of difficulties to get a chance to see the new child, unlimited numbers of people went to the house of Jagannātha Miśra to visit. Everyone packed their eyes to the brim with the charming vision of that sweet boy and seeing that beautiful sight, they forgot their own bodies. Narahari says that the divine appearance of the son of Śacī, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, in this way increased everyone’s affection and brought their hearts to the point of melting.”

Everyone was fully absorbed in the ecstasy of kīrtana day and night; no one could think of anything else. Finally it was time for maṅgala-arati, and the arati began. When the dancing and singing for maṅgala-arati was finished, the Vaiṣṇavas offered obeisances to one another and returned to their kutirs to take their morning baths. In this way, Śrī Jahnavā Mātā quickly took bath, and in order to cook for the Deities, she entered the kitchen. Being expert in the science of cooking, Śrī Jahnavā Mātā, within a short time had prepared various kinds of subjis and other vegetable dishes, sweets, cakes, and beverages. Śrīnivāsa Ācārya performed the *abhiṣeka* and the *pūjā* and offered the bhoga to the deities.

After the bhoga was offered and the bhoga-arati was finished, all the great souls present there were seated for taking mahāprasāda. Śrī Jahnavā Mātā distributed prasādam with her own hand to the assembled devotees. From time to time, a great sound of “Hari Hari!” filled the air as the great devotees assembled there, honored prasāda After all the mahānts were finished taking prasāda, Śrī Jahnavā Mātā requested Śrī Narottama, Śrīnivāsa and Śyāmānanda Prabhu to take prasāda. When everyone else was finished, Śrī Jahnavā Mātā herself took prasāda. Outside the kīrtana hall, King Santoṣa Datta distributed various kinds of *mahāprasāda t*o the full satisfaction of the thousands of people who had arrived for the festival. When all the friends and relatives of the *brāhmaṇas* who were present there, as well as all the unexpected and uninvited guests finally finished taking prasāda, then the King himself took mahāprasāda at home with his personal entourage. On the second day, Rāja Santoṣa Datta especially requested all the devotees to cook within their *kutirs* all different kinds of prasāda to be offered as before to the deities in a great feast.

On the third day, as the devotees in their places began to make preparations to leave, Rāja Santoṣa Datta with tears in his eyes gave the devotees gold coins, cloth, different kinds of waterpots, and offered all the devotees his respects. All the devotees bestowed many blessings upon the King and embraced him with great affection, before bidding him farewell and beginning their journeys home. Śrī Jahnavā Mātā, accompanied by her own entourage began her journey to Vṛndāvana. Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, and Śyāmānanda Prabhu remained behind in Kheturī Grama for a few days longer, and then they too left for their own homes, and bade farewell to Kheturī Gram. After the great festival in Kheturī, Śrīla Narottama Ṭhākura’s fame spread to the four directions. Rāmakṛṣṇa Ācārya and Gaṅganārāyaṇa Cakravartī as well as many other learned devotees throughout the area soon took shelter of the lotus feet of Śrīla Narottama Ṭhākura.

In the town of Gopāl Pura lived a *brāhmaṇa* named Śrī Vipra dāsa. One day, Narottama Ṭhākura suddenly arrived at his home. Vipra dāsa was extremely happy. He offered him a respectful welcome, offering him a seat and the hospitality of a humble *brāhmaṇa*, in accordance with Vedic tradition. In the place where Vipra dāsa had his rice paddy stored, a terrifying snake lived. Out of fear of the snake he didn’t dare go inside that storehouse. Vipra dāsa told Narottama dāsa of his fear. Hearing this, Narottama Ṭhākura smiled a little bit. He said, “Think nothing of it.” And when the Ṭhākura opened the door to the storehouse, the snake had vanished.” When Narottama came out of the storehouse, it could be seen that he had with him the deities of Viṣṇupriyā and Śrī Gaurasundara on his lap.” (Bhakti Ratnakara 10:202). Everyone saw a great miracle. Out of the storehouse Narottama Ṭhākura emerged holding deities of Śrī Gaurāṅga and Viṣṇupriyā on his lap. Taking those deities of Śrī Gaurāṅga-Viṣṇupriyā with him, Śrī Narottama Ṭhākura went to Kheturī Grama, where he installed them to be worshipped. At present those d eities are found in a place called Gambilat.

## 3: The Glories of Narottama

Once when Narottama Ṭhākura passed by a school, a learned smarta *brāhmaṇa*, insulted Narottama Ṭhākura in front of his students. Blaspheming him again and again, he called Narottama Ṭhākura a śūdra. After this offense, all the limbs of the *brāhmaṇa* began melting away from leprosy. Consumed by his incurable disease and seeing no hope for recovery the *brāhmaṇa* decided to throw himself in the Ganges and die. That night Durgā Devī came to the *brāhmaṇa* in a dream and said, “You fool. You have considered a great mahā-bhagavata, a pure devotee, to be a śūdra. Even if you die and take many millions of births you cannot be delivered. But if you simply go to him and beg forgiveness at his lotus feet, everything will be all right.”

The next day, in the afternoon, the *brāhmaṇa* went with a piece of cloth tied around his neck to indicate great humility, and in complete submission, weeping and weeping, fell before the lotus feet of Narottama Ṭhākura, begging his forgiveness. Gradually, as a result of his association with Narottama, his leprosy healed. Narottama Ṭhākura advised him to engage himself in kṛṣṇa bhajana. Gradually he became greatly devoted to Narottama Ṭhākura.

One day, Narottama Ṭhākura and Śrī Rāmacandra Kavirāja went to the Padmāvatī River to take bath. At that time they saw two young brāhmaṇas herding many goats and sheep. Narottama Ṭhākura asked the two brāhmaṇas if they were making their young lives successful by performing hari bhajana. The two young *brāhmaṇa* boys listened very attentively. Seeing the beautiful divine forms of Narottama Ṭhākura and Rāmacandra Kavirāja, and hearing their sweet words, the two brāhmaṇas approached the place where Narottama Ṭhākura and Śrī Rāmacandra Kavirāja stood on the banks of the river, and in a very humble mood offered their respectful prayers and obeisances. Narottama Ṭhākura asked them who they were and they introduced themselves, saying, “We come from the village of Goyasa Grama, and we are the sons of a Zamindara named Śivānanda Ācārya. Our names are Harināma and Rāmakṛṣṇa. In our house, at present, Durgā Pūjā is being conducted, and on the order of our father we are bringing all these goats and sheep to be slaughtered. Please give us some advice as to what we should do.”

Seeing the humble mood of the two sons of brāhmaṇas, Śrī Ṭhākura Mahāśaya smiled sweetly and began to speak as before on the truths of the Bhāgavata, explaining what the Vedas say about how karma-kaṇḍa is often performed in the modes of passion and ignorance, and how those whose minds are contaminated by the lower modes are candidates for hell. The Vedas explain that the practitioners of karma receive some piety and as a result go to the heavenly planets for short time, but having once gone to heaven for a short time, they fall into the hellish worlds to suffer. One who, being interested in material enjoyment, is captivated by the sweet words of the Vedas that promise material enjoyment, has his intelligence covered. Spending his life in material pursuits, such a person becomes a killer of animals, or a killer of the soul, and after death falls down into hell. All souls are the energy of Kṛṣṇa. One who sees the Paramātmā everywhere, who is free from violence to others, who has no false ego, and who always worships the Supreme Lord becomes free from repeated birth and death and attains the liberated position of divine service at the lotus feet of the Lord.”

Hearing these words from the mouth of Śrīla Narottama Ṭhākura, the two *brāhmaṇa* boys fell before his holy feet and said, “Please bless these most fallen young brāhmaṇas with the dust of your lotus feet.” The Thakura then touched his hand to their heads, saying, “May you attain kṛṣṇa-bhakti.” At this, the young *brāhmaṇa* boys set the goats and sheep free, bathed in the Padma River, and went with Śrī Rāmacandra Kavirāja and Narottama Ṭhākura to the temple of Śrīman Mahāprabhu. That day, after taking prasāda they again heard from Śrī Narottama Ṭhākura and Rāmacandra Kavirāja about different aspects of the Absolute Truth. The next day, after shaving their heads, the two young brāhmaṇas took initiation in the rādhā-kṛṣṇa mantra. Harināma accepted the mantra from Rāmacaṅdra Kavirāja and Rāmakṛṣṇa from Narottama Ṭhākura.

After some time, Śivānanda Ācārya began searching and searching for his sons until he found that they had been seen in Kheturī Grama where they were living as disciples in the āśrāma of Narottama Ṭhākura. Śivānanda Ācārya could not contain his anger. A few days later, the two brothers returned home. Their foreheads were marked with the tilaka of a Vaiṣṇava, their throats decorated with tulasī mālā, the twelve parts of their bodies marked with viṣṇu tilaka, their heads shaved, wearing the śika of a devotee of Kṛṣṇa. Seeing all this Śivananda raged with the hot fury of an uncontrolled fire. He told them: “O fools! Where does it say in the śastra that the position of a Vaiṣṇava is greater than the position of a *brāhmaṇa*, or that by becoming a Vaiṣṇava one is better than a *brāhmaṇa*? You have neglected Durgā for so long, but your life is useless without Durgā. Moreover, if the Vaiṣṇavas make disciples out of brāhmaṇas it will lead to chaos in society; the brāhmaṇas will lose their position, and if the Paṇḍitas are defeated, religion will be destroyed and everything will be lost.”

Hearing these words of their father, the two young *brāhmaṇa* boys spoke as follows: “On the other hand, that “religion” or “right work” which causes violence to others, and ends only in misery, cannot properly be called religion or proper activity. Rather it must be called irreligious and wrong activity. O Father! Giving up the worship of śalagrama-nārāyaṇa, why have you established the worship of Durgā-devī and other demigods? Giving careful thought to the conclusions surrounding the performance of śrī nārāyaṇa-bhajana you should understand the worship of Śiva and Durgā to be a useless waste of time.”

Śivananda Ācārya and *smarta paṇḍitas* were thus defeated by the conclusions given by the *brāhmaṇa* boys. Śivananda began to deeply consider: “If a great Paṇḍita were here, he could defeat all this and belittle the position of *vaiṣṇava-dharma* by counter-argument and word jugglery.” Śivananda Ācārya brought a great mahā-paṇḍita from Mithila named Murari. At that time, for the purpose of having a debate, he called for his sons and told them that he wanted them to explain before a public assembly their conclusions that a Vaiṣṇava is better than a *brāhmaṇa*. Śrī Harināma and Śrī Rāmakṛṣṇa remembering the lotus feet of their guru mahārāja explained the siddhānta or conclusions of the Bhāgavatam, and the arguments of the smartas were so utterly cut to pieces that when it was his turn to speak the great smarta mahā-paṇḍita Murari was unable to put forward any arguments in response. When it was all over, the mahā-paṇḍita fled the assembly in great embarassment with his head held low and accepted the occupation of a beggar.

That night, as he lay defeated, Śivānanda Ācārya began to meditate on Durgā Devī. Gradually he fell asleep and Durgā Devī appeared to him in a dream. At that time she said: “O Śivānanda! Śrī Hari is the master of everyone. Those whom He covers with ignorance turn to my worship. And I assure the destruction of those who worship me. Those who are not Kṛṣṇa-minded are demons. On the other hand, those who are dear devotees of Kṛṣṇa are really dear to me. For your own sake, if you want to become free from the offenses you have committed and attain auspiciousness, you had better go to Narottama Ṭhākura and beg forgiveness from his lotus feet. Otherwise I will destroy you, you offender of Vaiṣṇavas!” After speaking these words of chastisement to Śivānanda Ācārya, Durgā Devī disappeared.

Gangānārāyaṇa Chakravartī was a famous and highly learned *brāhmaṇa* living in Gambhila Grama. He heard from the lips of Narottama Ṭhākura the siddhānta of the Gosvāmīs with great attention and took shelter of the lotus feet of Narottama, where he later studied very deeply the scriptures of the Gosvāmīs.

There was another *brāhmaṇa* named Jagannātha Ācārya who was a worshipper of Durgā. One day in a dream Durgā appeared to him and said, “O you simple *brāhmaṇa*, go to Narottama Ṭhākura. Take shelter of his lotus feet. Practice kṛṣṇa bhajana and you will achieve the highest good fortune. Kṛṣṇa is my master and my guru. Not a blade of grass moves without His wish.” Jagannātha Ācārya, after his afternoon bath, went to Kheturī Grama and offered his danḍavats at the lotus feet of Narottama Ṭhākura, explaining all that Durgā had told him in the dream. Hearing this, the Ṭhākura smiled slightly and said, “You are very fortunate to have attained Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.” One auspicious day the Ṭhākura initiated him in the *rādhā-kṛṣṇa mantra*. Śrī Jagannātha Ācārya became a very dear and confidential disciple of Narottama Ṭhākura.

Seeing the glories of Śrīla Narottama Ṭhākura, the society of smarta brāhmaṇas roasted with envy. A large faction of them went to the Rāja Nṛsiṁha and petitioned him for redress. They told him: “Mahārāja! If you don’t rescue the *brāhmaṇas*, your reputation will be ruined and your death will be assured. The son of Rāja Kṛṣṇānanda Datta, Narottama Ṭhākura, is a śūdra, and yet he dares to make disciples of the brāhmaṇas. If this goes on, we shall all be drowned like the members of the Yadu dynasty.” Rāja Nṛsiṁha said, “I shall protect you. But please tell me, what is to be done?”

The brāhmaṇas said; “We shall all go to Kheturī Grama along with the great and famous, the world-conquering scholar mahā-digvijaya-paṇḍita Śrī Rūpa Nārāyaṇa and defeat Narottama. With that great Paṇḍita as our leader, Narottama will not be able to say anything. You please assist us in all this.” The King, Rāja Nṛsiṁha said, “I myself shall accompany you every step of the way.” In this way, that group of *brāhmaṇas* along with the great and learned world-conquering Paṇḍita Rūpa Nārāyaṇa began their journey to Kheturī Grama. As they were traveling on the road someone heard the news and went to Kheturī Grama where he informed Śrīla Rāmacandra Kavirāja and Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura.

When Śrī Rāmacandra Kavirāja and Śrī Gangānārāyaṇa Cakravartī heard all these, they became very disturbed. Thereafter, after some inquiry, the two of them came to know that the party of smarta paṅḍitas, were about to arrive in the marketplace of a town called Kumāra Pura, and that after resting there for one day, they would reach Kheturī Grama the following day. Rāmacandra and Gaṅgānārāyaṇa quickly went to that marketplace in Kumāra-pura and the two of them set up two different shops. Śrī Rāmacandra Kavirāja set up a stall for selling clay pots and Gaṅganārāyaṇa Cakravartī set himself up in a stall selling pān and betel-nut.

The *smarta paṇḍitas*, along with Rāja Nṛsiṁha, arrived in the marketplace of Kumāra-pura and set up their camp near the shops. The disciples of the Paṇḍitas went to buy some clay pots for cooking with, and went to the pottery shop. The potter (who was Rāmacandra Kavirāja) began speaking to them in pure Saṅskrit. The disciples of the Paṅḍitas began speaking in Sanskrit also, and soon they began arguing back and forth in Sanskrit and the disciples of the Paṇḍitas were defeated. In the same way, when the same students went to purchase *pān* and betel-nuts from the shop of the pan-walla (Gaṅgānārāyaṇa Cakravartī) he spoke to them in pure Sanskrit. They again began to argue. Gradually their teachers arrived at the place where the argument was going on and found themselves unable to reply to the arguments of the pan-walla and potter.

Finally King Rāja Nṛsiṁha arrived on the scene with the great Paṇḍita Rūpa Nārāyaṇa. At that time a great uproar of argumentation filled the four directions. In the presence of the king, the potter and the pān-walla defeated all the smarta brāhmaṇas, including Rūpa Nārāyaṇa. Rāja Nṛsiṁha made some inquiries and came to know that the pān-walla and the potter were disciples of Narottama dāsa. At that time he said to the Paṇḍitas: “When you are unable to defeat a common, ordinary disciple of Narottama in the matter of siddhānta, how will you defeat Narottama himself?” The *smarta paṇḍitas* were silent. Realizing their defeat, they made preparations to return to their own villages.

That evening, King Rāja Nṛsiṁha and Śrī Rūpa Nārayaṇa saw Durgā Devī in a dream. She told them, “If you don’t accept the shelter of Narottama’s lotus feet, I will cut you all to pieces with my sharpened sword.” The following morning Rāja Nṛsiṁha and Rūpa Nārāyaṇa arrived at the place of Narottama Ṭhākura. Narottama Ṭhākura received them with great affection and with all due respect and cordiality, offering them a seat. He said, “I am very fortunate to have the company of such highly learned and noble personages such as your good selves.” Rāja Nṛsiṁha and Rūpa Nārāyaṇa were overwhelmed at Narottama’s polite and gentle Vaiṣṇava behavior and fell prostrate at his lotus feet begging forgiveness for their offenses. At length, upon hearing of the warning that Durgā Devī had given them, Narottama smiled very sweetly. Thereafter, within a few days, he initiated them in the *rādhā-kṛṣṇa mantra*.

## 4: The Disappearance of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura

Śrīla Narottama Ṭhākura was constantly absorbed in singing the glories of Śrī Gaurāṅga and Nityānanda. Day after day many atheists, agnostics, offenders, Śiva worshippers, followers of Durgā, dry logicians, mental speculators and *karmīs* were purified by the touch of his holy lotus feet. Taking the blessings of Narottama, Śrī Rāmacandra Kavirāja went to Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma. After a few months there, he entered into the eternal *lila* of Śrī Rādhā and Govinda. Śrīla Śrīnivāsa Ācārya had already departed and entered into eternal *līlā* of Śrī Rādhā and Govinda.

Hearing all these terrible news, Śrīla Narottama plunged into the ocean of separation where he began to drown. Barely able to keep afloat in the ocean of separation, Śrīla Narottama Ṭhākura went near the banks of the Ganges in the village called Gambhilaya and entered a temple of Mahāprabhu. Narottama ordered the devotees to perform *kīrtana*. The devotees began performing *saṅkīrtana*. After *saṅkīrtana*, Narottama Ṭhākura went to the river’s edge, and with tears in his eyes took *darśana* of the Ganges, offering his obeisances again and again. At that time, he entered the waters of the Ganges. After entering a short distance into the water he requested all the devotees to fill the four directions with the loud chanting of the holy name. At that time, Śrī Rāmakṛṣṇa Ācārya and Śrī Gaṅgānārāyaṇa Cakravartī began *kīrtanas* in two different places. In the midst of all these, the Ṭhākura told the two of them, “Pour the waters of the Ganges over my body.” Saying this, everyone was immersed in the waves of *saṅkīrtana*. As the *kīrtana* went on, they were just ready to pour Ganges water over the body of Śrī Narottama Ṭhākura, when just at that moment, Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, who was absorbed in chanting the holy name, merged into the waters of the Ganges and vanished from worldly view! His disappearance day is celebrated on the *kṛṣṇa pañcamī* day of the month of *Kartika*.

## 5: Prārthanā

## Song 1—Lālasā-mayī (Longing for direct service to the Lord)

*gaurāṅga’ balite habe pulaka-śarīra*  
*‘hari hari’ balite nayane ba’he nīra*  
*āra kabe nitāi-cāṅdera karuṇā ha-ibe*  
*saṁsāra-vāsanā mora kabe tuccha ha’be*  
*viṣaya chāḍiya kabe śuddha ha’be mana*  
*kabe hāma heraba śrī vṛndāvana*  
*rūpa-raghunātha-pade haibe ākuti*  
*kabe hāma būjhāba se jugala pīriti*  
*rūpa-raghunātha-pade rahu mora āśa*  
*prārthanā karaye sadā narottama dāsa*

**Пословный перевод:**

*gaurāṅga—*Lord Gaurāṅga; *balite—*to chant; *habe—*will be; *pulaka-śarīra—*shivering of the whole body; *‘hari hari—*Lord Hari; *balite—*to chant; *nayane—*in the eyes; *ba’he—*will flow; *nīra—*tears; *āra kabe—*and when will; *nitāi-cāṅdera—*of Lord Nityānanda; *karuṇā—*mercy; *haibe—*will be; *saṁsāra—*material existence; *vāsanā*—desires; *mora—*mine*; kabe—*when; *tuccha—*insignificant; *ha’be—*will be; *viṣaya—*material enjoyments; *chāḍiya—*giving up; *kabe—*when; *śuddha—*pure; *ha’be—*will be; *mana—*mind; *kabe—*when; *hāma—*I; *heraba—*will see; *śrī vṛndāvana—*Śrī Vṛndāvana; *rūpa-raghunātha-pade—*unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Rūpa and Raghunātha Gosvāmīs; *haibe—*will be; *ākuti—*eager; *kabe—*when; *hāma—*I; *būjhāba—*will understand; *se—*that; *jugala pīriti—*the loving pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa; *rūpa-raghunātha-pade—*unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Rūpa and Raghunātha Gosvāmīs; *rahu—*let them remain; *mora—*mine; *āśa—*desires; *prārthanā karaye—*prays; *sadā—*always; *narottama dāsa—*Narottama dāsa.

**Комментарий:**

This is a song sung by Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, a great devotee and Ācārya in the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava Sampradāya, the disciplic succession coming down from Lord Caitanya. Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has written many songs, which are recognised as authoritative by all the Vaiṣṇavas. He has sung these songs in simple Bengali language, but the purport***—***the deep meaning of his songs***—***is very significant.

In this song, he says; “When will that day come that I shall simply sing Lord Caitanya’s name and there will be shivering on my body?” *Gaurāṅga balite habe pulaka-śarīra. Pulaka-śarīra* means “shivering on the body”. When one is factually situated in the transcendental platform, sometimes there are eight kinds of symptoms: crying, talking like a madman, and shivering of the body and dancing without any care for any other men. These symptoms develop automatically. They are not practised artificially. So Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura is aspiring for that day, not that one has to artificially imitate. He does not recommend that. He says; “When that day will come, so that simply by uttering the name of Lord Caitanya there will be a shivering on my body?” *Gaurāṅga balite habe pulaka-śarīra*. And *hari hari balite*: “As soon as I shall chant ‘Hari Hari,’ or ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa,’ there will be pouring down of tears from my eyes.” *hari hari balite nayane bahe nīra*. *Nīra* means water. Similarly, Caitanya Mahāprabhu also said, “When will that day come?” We should simply aspire. But if, by Kṛṣṇa’s grace, we reach that stage, these symptoms will come automatically. But Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura says that it is not possible to reach that stage without being freed from material affection.

Therefore he says, āra kabe nitāi-caṅder, karuṇā na haibe: “When will that day come when Lord Nityānanda’s mercy will be bestowed upon me” *āra kabe nitāi-caṅdera karuṇā na hai-be, saṁsāra-vāsanā mora kabe tuccha ha’be. Saṁsāra-vāsanā*means “the desire for material enjoyment.” *Saṁsāra-bāsanā mora kabe tuccha ha’be*: “When my desire for material enjoyment will be insignificant, non-important.’’ Tuccha. Tuccha means “a thing which we calculate to be of no value”. Similarly, spiritual advancement is possible when one is convinced that this material world and material happiness is of no value. It cannot give me any real bliss of life. This conviction is very much necessary. Saṁsāra-vāsanā mora kabe tuccha ha’be. And he also says, “When I shall be free from the desires of material enjoyment, then it will be possible to see the real nature of Vṛndāvana.” viṣaya chāḍiyā kabe śuddha ha’be mana: “When my mind will be completely purified, uncontaminated from the material contamination, at that time it will be possible for me to see what Vṛndāvana is.” In other words, one cannot go to Vṛndāvana by force and live there and expect to achieve that transcendental bliss. No. One has to make his mind freed from all material desires, then one can live at Vṛndāvana and relish its residential benefit. So Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura says viṣaya chāḍiyā kabe, śuddha ha’be mana “When my mind will be freed from the contamination of this material enjoyment and I shall be purified, then it will be possible for me to see Vṛndāvana as it is.” Otherwise it is not possible.

And he says again that, to go to Vṛndāvana means to understand the transcendental pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. How will this be possible? So he says, *rūpa-raghunātha-pade haibe ākuti*. Rūpa Gosvāmī, beginning from Rūpa Gosvāmī down to Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī, there were six Gosvāmīs: Rūpa, Sanātana, Gopāla Bhaṭṭa, Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa, Jīva Gosvāmī, Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī. So he says, *rūpa-raghunātha-pade:* “Beginning from Rūpa Gosvāmī down to Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī,” *pade*, “at their lotus feet. When I shall be eager to be attached at their lotus feet;” *Rūpa-raghunātha-pade, haibe ākuti. Ākuti,* eagerness. What is that eagerness? That means to understand Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa through the guidance of the Gosvāmīs. One should not try to understand Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa by his own effort. That will not help him. As these Gosvāmīs have given us direction, just like in *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*. So one has to follow step after step how to make progress. Then there will be a fortunate day when we shall be able to understand what are the pastimes of loving affairs between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Otherwise, if we take it as ordinary boy and girls reciprocating their loving feelings, then we will misunderstand. Then there will be the products of *prakṛta-sahajiyā,* victims of Vṛndāvana.

So Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura is giving us the direction how we can reach the highest perfectional stage of associating with Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. The first thing is that one should be very much attached to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. That will lead us. Because He came to deliver the understanding of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, therefore one should first of all surrender to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. By surrendering to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Nityānanda Prabhu will be pleased, and by His being pleased, we shall be freed from material desires. And when material desires are no more, then we shall be able to enter Vṛndāvana. And after entering Vṛndāvana, when we are eager to serve the six Gosvāmīs, then we can reach the platform to understand the pastimes of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

## Song 2—Saṁprārthanātmikā (Prayer for the awakening of Attachment to the Lord)

*rādhā-kṛṣṇa! nivedana ei jana kare*  
*doṅha ati rasamaya sa karuṇa-hṛdaya*  
*avadhāna kara nātha more*  
*he kṛṣṇa gokulacandra he gopī prāṇa-vallabha*  
*he kṛṣṇa-priyā –śiromaṇi*  
*hema-gaurī śyāma-gāya śravaṇe paraśa pāya*  
*guṇa śuni juḍāya parāṇī*  
*adhama-durgati-jane kevala karuṇā mane*  
*tribhuvana e jaśa-kheyāti*  
*śuniya sādhura mukhe śaraṇa lainū sukhe*  
*upekṣile nāhi mora gati*  
*jaya rādhe jaya kṛṣṇa jaya jaya rādhe kṛṣṇa*  
*kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa jaya jaya rādhe*  
*aṅjali mastake dhari narottama bhūme paḍi*  
*kahe doṅhe purāo mana-sādhe*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rādhā-kṛṣṇa!—*O Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa; *nivedana—*request; *ei—*this; *jana—*person; *kare*—does; *doṅhe—*both; *ati—*too much; *rasamaya—*embodiment of transcendental mellows; *sa karuṇā-hṛdaya—*kind-hearted; *avadhāna—*glance; *kara*—please do; *nātha—*O my Lord; *more—*to me; *he kṛṣṇa—*O Lord Kṛṣṇa; *gokulacandra—*the moon of Gokula; *he gopī prāṇa-vallabha—*O life and soul of the gopīs; *he kṛṣṇa-priyā—*O beloved of Kṛṣṇa; *śiromaṇi—*the crest jewel; *hema-gaurī—*golden-complexioned Rādhā; *śyāma—*Śyāmasundara; *gāya—*sing; *śravaṇe—*by hearing; *paraśa pāya—*gets realisation; *guṇa—*qualities; *śuni—*hearing; *juḍaya—*relief; *parāṇī—*soul; *adhama—*fallen; *durgati-jane—*wretched person; *kevala—*only*; karuṇā—*compassion; *mane—*in the mind; *tribhuvana—*in the three worlds; *e jaśa-khyāti—*your fame; *śuniya—*having heard; *sadhūra—*of the devotees; *mukhe—*through the mouths; *śaraṇa-lainu—*took shelter; *sukhe—*happily; *upekṣile—*if you neglect; *nāhi—*not; *mora—*mine; *gati—*alternative; *jaya rādhe—*All glories to Rādhā; *jaya kṛṣṇa—*all glories to Kṛṣṇa; *jaya jaya rādhe kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa kṛṣṇa jaya jaya rādhe aṅjali—*folded hands; *mastake dhari—*touching the head; *narottama—*Narottama dāsa; *bhūme-paḍi—*falling on the ground; *kahe—*says; *doṅhe*—both; *purāo—*fulfill; *mana-sādhe—*my heart’s desire.

**Перевод:**

**O Rādhā! O Kṛṣṇa! Who are both full of transcendental mellows, so charming and so kind-hearted and merciful. I am offering this prayer before You, and praying that You may glance mercifully on me.**

**O dark-complexioned Kṛṣṇa, moon of Gokula, and the beloved of the gopīs, O golden Rādhā, the crown-jewel among all of Kṛṣṇa’s dearest ones, only when I hear and chant Your transcendental names does my heart become pacified.**

**You are well-known throughout the three worlds for being so merciful to the fallen and distressed souls. When I heard about Your glories from saintly persons, I eagerly surrendered myself to You. Now If You ignore me, what will I do? I have no other place to go.**

**Again and again I repeat O Rādhā, all glories to You! O Kṛṣṇa all glories to You! Narottama dāsa thus folds his hands placing them on his forehead, and falling to the ground like a stick before You, prays, “Please fulfill my innermost heart’s desire.”**

## Song 3—Dainya-bodhika Prārthanā (Prayer Describing Fallen Condition)

*hari hari! ki mora karama gati manda*  
*braje rādhā-kṛṣṇa pada nā sevinu tila-ādha*  
*nā bhūjhinū rāgera sambandha*  
*svarūpa sanātana rūpa raghūnātha bhaṭṭa-yūgau*  
*bhūgarbha śrī-jīva lokanātha*  
*ihāṅ sabāra pāda-padma nā sevinū tila-ādha*  
*kise āra puribeka sādha*  
*kṛṣṇadāsa kavirāja rasika bhakata-mājha*  
*je racila caitanya-carita*  
*gaura-govinda-līlā śunile galaye śilā*  
*nā ḍūvila tāhe mora cita*  
*tāṅhāra bhaktera-saṅga tāṅra saṅge yāṅra saṅga*  
*tāra saṅge kena nāila bāsa*  
*ki mora dūḥkhera kathā janama goṅāinū vṛthā*  
*dhik dhik narottama dāsa*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hari hari*—O Lord Hari; *ki—*what; *mora—*mind; *karama—*activities; *gati*—destinations; *manda*—unfortunate; *braje—*in Braja; *rādhā-kṛṣṇa pada—*the lotus feet of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa; *nā—*not; *sevinū—*serve; *tila-ādha—*even for a moment; *nā bhūjhinū—*did not understand; *rāgera—*of attachment; *sambandha—*relationship; *svarūpa—*Śrīla Svarūpa Gośvāmī; *sanātana—*Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī*; rūpa—*Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī; *raghunātha—*Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī; *bhaṭṭa-yūgau—*Śrīla Gopal Bhaṭṭa and Śrīla Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmīs; *bhūgarbha*—Śrīla Bhūgarbha Gosvāmī; *śrī-jīva*—Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī; *lokanātha—*Śrīla Lokanātha Gosvāmī; *ihaṅ sābara—*all of them; *pāda-padma—*lotus feet; *nā sevinā*—did not serve; *tila-ādha—*even for a moment; *kise āra—*how; *puribeka—*will fulfill; *sādha—*desire; *kṛṣṇadāsa kavirāja—*Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī; *rasika—*loving dealings; *bhakta-mājha—*among the devotees; *je—*who; *racila*—composed; *caitanya-carita—*Śrī Caitanya Caritāmṛta; *gaura-govinda-līlā—*the pastimes of Śrī Caitanya and Śrī Kṛṣṇa; *śunile—*by hearing; *galaye*—melts; *śilā*—stones; *nā*—not; *ḍūvila—*merge; *tāhe—*in those pastimes; *mora cita—*my hearts; *taṅhara—*his; *bhaktera—*of the devotees; *saṅga—*association; *taṅra—*his; *saṅge—*in the association; *yaṅra—*who; *saṅga—*associate*s; tānra—*his; *saṅge—*in the association; *kena—*why; *nāhila bāsa—*did not reside; *ki—*what; *mora—*mind; *duḥkhera—*distressful; *kathā—*topics; *janama*—human form of life; *goñāinu—*spent; *vṛthā—*useless; *dhik dhik—*shame! shame!; *narottama dāsa—*Narottama dāsa.

**Перевод:**

**O Lord Hari, how unfortunate I am. Due to my past misdeeds I have never worshipped the lotus feet of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa even for a moment, nor have I been able to undestand Their transcendental loving relationship.**

**Not even for half a moment have I served the lotus feet of Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, Śrīla Sanātana Gosvāmī, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī, Śrīla Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī, Śrīla Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī, Śrīla Bhūgarbha Gosvāmī, Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī, or Śrīla Lokanātha Gosvāmī. How then will it be possible for my desire to be fulfilled?**

**Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, the devotee who knows very well the loving relationships of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, compiled Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta and Govinda-līlāmṛta, describing the nectarine pastimes of Śrī Gaura-Govinda. Even the stones melt in ecstasy upon hearing these narrations. But alas! Only my mind is not attracted to them.**

**Furthermore, bereft of association with the friends and companions of these great souls, I have spent my life uselessly. This is the sad story of my life. What a shameless life I, Narottama dāsa, have led.**

## Song 4—hari hari! biphale

*hari hari! biphale janama goñāinu*  
*manuṣya-janama pāiya rādhā-kṛṣṇa nā bhajiyā*  
*jāniyā śuniyā biṣa khāinu*  
*golokera prema-dhana hari-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*rati nā janmila kena tāya*  
*saṁsāra-biṣānale dibā-niśi hiyā jvale*  
*juḍāite nā kainu upāya*  
*brajendra-nandana jei śacī-suta haila sei*  
*balarāma haila nitāi*  
*dīna-hīna jata chila hari-nāme uddhārila*  
*tāra sākṣi jagāi mādhāi*  
*hā hā prabhu nanda-suta bṛṣabhanū-sutā-juta*  
*karunā karaha ei-bāra*  
*narottama-dāsa kaya nā ṭheliha rāṅgā pāya*  
*tomā bine ke āche āmāra*

**Пословный перевод:**
